The Aerospace & Defence, Industrial, and Medtech segments continued to demonstrate strong momentum, delivering 
a combined year on year growth of 43%, driven primarily by increased business from leading customers. Within the 
Aerospace segment, the Company’s top two customers recorded a growth of 13% during FY 2025–26, underscoring the 
strength of long standing client relationships.
In contrast, the Defence segment witnessed a decline during the year, largely attributable to the completion of larger 
orders in FY 2024–25. The Other segment registered growth, supported by the addition of new customers in the 
Automobile sector during FY 2025–26.
PRODUCT-WISE PERFORMANCE
PCBA, Box Build, and Mechanical assemblies continued to be the Company’s key product categories during FY 2025–26. 
The PCBA segment witnessed a decline during the year, primarily on account of the completion of a large order in FY 
2024–25. In contrast, the Box Build business increased its share of revenues, supported by new orders received from key 
customers.
The Mechanical & Other segment, comprising primarily Precision Machining, recorded growth during the year and was 
further strengthened by the addition of the B2S vertical to the Company’s business portfolio.

FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE WITH KEY RATIOS
The financial performance of Cyient DLM Limited discussed below has been prepared in accordance with Indian 
Accounting Standards (Ind AS) and relates to the consolidated financial results of Cyient DLM Limited and its 
subsidiary. This discussion should be read in conjunction with the Consolidated Financial Statements and the 
accompanying notes forming part of this Annual Report for the financial year ended 31 March 2026.
Revenue performance during 
FY 2025–26 was driven primarily 
by growth in the Aerospace and 
Industrial segments, with major 
customers in these segments 
recording growth in excess of 12% 
year on year. The Defence segment, 
however, witnessed a decline in 
percentage growth, largely due to 
the completion of a large order in FY 
2024–25.
Other Income for FY 2025–26 
amounted to C 362.63 mn, compared 
to C 261.57 mn in FY 2024–25. The 
increase was primarily attributable 
to one-off earn out reversals 
aggregating C 195.75 mn recognised 
during the year.
Direct Material Costs (Cost of 
Raw Materials and Change in 
Inventories) as a percentage of 
revenue stood at 59.63% in FY 2025–
26, compared to 72.9% in FY 2024–
25. The reduction was mainly driven 
by a favourable shift in revenue mix 
and the execution of strategic supply 
chain initiatives.
In addition, the integration of Altek 
Electronics, which operates with a 
relatively lower direct material cost 
structure, and the inclusion of the 
B2S segment during FY 2025–26 
contributed to the reduction in direct 
material cost as a proportion of 
revenue in the current year.
Employee Benefit Expenses 
increased to 18.0% of revenue in 
FY 2025–26, compared to 12.3% 
in FY 2024–25, representing an 
increase of 5.7 percentage points. 
This rise is primarily attributable to 
the integration of Altek Electronics, 
which has a cost structure 
characterised by lower material costs 
and higher employee expenses.
The increase in absolute terms was 
mainly due to:
l Salaries and Wages (including 
bonus): An increase of 23% 
(C 358.93 mn) over FY 2024–25, 
largely arising from the full 
year impact of Altek Electronics 
integration, in addition to normal 
wage inflation in the base 
business.
l Staff Welfare and Other Expenses: 
An increase of C 136.93, primarily 
attributable to the integration of 
Altek Electronics Inc.
Finance Costs for the year stood 
at C 271.69 mn, reflecting a 27.6% 
compared to FY 2024–25. The decline 
was driven by a combination of lower 
average borrowing levels and softer 
interest rates during the year.
Depreciation and Amortisation 
expenses, as a percentage of 
revenue, increased to 3.4% in FY 
2025–26, compared to 2.2% in FY 
2024–25. While depreciation and 
amortisation increased by 25.5% 
in absolute terms, the higher ratio 
to revenue primarily reflects lower 
revenue absorption during the year, 
despite improved efficiency in the 
utilisation of fixed assets.
Other Expenses comprise stores and 
spares, power and fuel, repairs and 
maintenance, travel and conveyance, 
and B2S segment staffing costs, 
among others. These expenses 
increased by C 676.37 mn, or 76.8%, 
compared to FY 2024–25, primarily 
due to scale up of operations and the 
inclusion of new business segments 
during the year.
Taxes, The ETR for FY 2025–26 stood 
at 21.3%, down 450 basis points 
from 25.8% in FY 2024–25. The 
current-year rate was lowered by 
a non-taxable gain on the reversal 
of the Altek Electronics earnout, 
which lifted profit before tax without 
an equivalent tax charge. On a 
normalised basis   excluding the 
earnout reversal this year and the 
acquisition-related items recognised 
last year  the underlying ETR was 
broadly stable across the two 
periods.

equity shares.

in equity during the year was

LIABILITIES
Borrowings, total borrowings 
declined significantly from C 2,437.56 
mn in FY 2024–25 to C 1,061.25 mn 
in FY 2025–26. During the year, the 
Company reduced outstanding 
balances relating to inter company 
loans, term loans, and working 
capital borrowings, reflecting 
strengthened balance sheet position 
and disciplined Working Capital 
management.
Trade payables, primarily comprise 
amounts payable to suppliers for 
the purchase of goods and services. 
Trade payables stood at C 2,733.14 
mn as at 31 March 2026, compared 
to C 2,498.83 mn as at 31 March 2025. 
The increase of C 234.31mn was 
mainly due to extended credit terms 
negotiated with suppliers.
OTHER CURRENT LIABILITIES
Other current liabilities stood at 
C 994.27 mn as at 31 March 2026, 
as compared to C 1,160.80 mn as 
at 31 March 2025. These primarily 
represent advances received from 
customers and unearned revenue. 
The reduction during the year was 
largely attributable to reduction of 
Customer Advances received.
ASSETS
Non Current Assets, overall, non 
current assets stood at C328.83 mn 
as on 31 March 2026, compared 
to C 97.46 mn as of 31 March 2025, 
representing an increase of C 231.37 
mainly due to advance paid for 
procurement of land. Investments 
classified as financial assets 
declined by C 207.16 mn, reflecting 
a fair value adjustment in the 
Company’s investment in STUAM 
Technologies (formerly Innovation 
Communications Systems).
CURRENT ASSETS

as of 31 March 2026, compared 
to C 5,712.73 mn as of 31 March 
2025, representing an increase of 
C 760.59 mn. Inventory levels were 
elevated primarily due to advance 
procurement of critical raw materials, 
undertaken in response to global 
supply chain disruptions, including 
the geopolitical situations in West 
Asia and shortages of certain critical 
components (such as memory 
products).

C 3,473.97 mn as at 31 March 2025 to 
C 3,073.12 mn as at 31 March 2026, 
representing a reduction of C 400.85 
mn. The decline was primarily 
attributable to lower revenue levels 
during FY 2025–26.
Cash and Bank Balances declined 
by C 1619.82 mn during the year, 
mainly due to the utilisation of IPO 
proceeds in line with the stated 
objects of the issue. The composition 
of cash and cash equivalents is 
summarised below:

RISK MANAGEMENT
The Company has an organisation 
wide Enterprise Risk Management 
(ERM) framework aligned with 
leading industry standards. The 
framework provides a structured 
approach to identifying, assessing, 
and mitigating risks across the 
Company’s operations and covers key 
risk categories including strategic, 
reputational, operational, financial, 
and compliance/legal risks. 
The ERM framework is subject 
to periodic review by KPMG, 
the Company’s Internal Auditor. 
Reports on risk assessment and 
mitigation status are presented 
to the Risk Committee, enabling 
effective oversight and monitoring. 
In addition, the Company has 
constituted an internal Risk 
Committee, which periodically 
reviews the effectiveness of the risk 
management processes and ensures 
that appropriate mitigation measures 
are in place.

INTERNAL CONTROL 
SYSTEMS AND THEIR 
ADEQUACY
The Company has established 
adequate internal control systems 
commensurate with its size, scale, 
and the nature of its operations. 
These systems are designed to 
provide reasonable assurance 
regarding the integrity and reliability 
of financial and operational 
information, compliance with 
applicable statutes and regulations, 
safeguarding of assets against 
unauthorised use or loss, execution 
of transactions with appropriate 
authorisation, and adherence to 
established corporate policies and 
procedures.
The Company has a well defined 
Delegation of Authority framework, 
which governs the approval of 
revenue and expenditure. A global 
SAP ERP system is deployed across 
operations to support accounting, 
consolidation, and management 
reporting, enabling seamless 
data integration and information 
exchange across locations.
For FY 2025–26, the Company 
appointed M/s KPMG as its Internal 
Auditors. The internal audit was 
conducted in accordance with a risk 
based audit plan, which is reviewed 
annually in consultation with the 
Statutory Auditors, M/s S.R. Batliboi 
& Associates LLP , and approved by 
the Audit Committee. The scope of 
internal audit covers all significant 
operational and financial areas, with 
a focus on evaluating the adequacy 
and effectiveness of internal control 
systems.
The Audit Committee of the Board, 
details of which are provided in 
the Corporate Governance Report, 
reviews the internal audit findings 
and monitors the implementation 
of agreed corrective actions. The 
Committee also engages with 
the Statutory Auditors to, inter 
alia, obtain their views on the 
adequacy and effectiveness of the 
Company’s internal control systems 
and periodically reports significant 
observations to the Board of 
Directors.
Further, the Statutory Auditors have 
independently audited the internal 
financial controls over financial 
reporting as at 31 March 2026 and 
have issued an unmodified opinion, 
confirming that adequate internal 
financial controls were in place and 
that such controls were operating 
effectively.
SHAREHOLDER’S 
VALUE CREATION
Following the successful Initial Public 
Offering (IPO) in FY 2023–24, Cyient 
DLM created meaningful value for 
its shareholders, underpinned by 
sustained growth in revenues and 
profitability during the post listing 
period. The Company’s operating 
performance and strategic execution 
reinforced its long-term value 
proposition for investors.
During the year, the market 
price of the Company’s equity 
shares declined by 34.8% as of 
1 April 2026 when compared to 
the corresponding period in the 
previous year. This movement largely 
reflects broader industry wide and 
market related factors, with several 
peer companies experiencing 
similar trends in their stock price 
performance during the same period.
The Company has fully utilised 
the IPO proceeds in line with the 
objects stated in the Red Herring 
Prospectus (RHP). The proceeds 
were applied towards repayment of 
borrowings, capital expenditure, and 
funding incremental working capital 
requirements, thereby strengthening 
the balance sheet and supporting 
long-term operational growth.
As of 31 March 2026, utilisation of 
IPO proceeds stands at 100%, and 
the designated monitoring account 
has been duly closed.

INVESTOR ENGAGEMENT
The Company engages with the 
investor community in a structured, 
regular, and transparent manner 
to communicate its business 
outlook, strategic priorities, 
and new initiatives. Cyient DLM 
recognises that effective and open 
communication with investors is 
integral to long-term value creation, 
alongside consistent financial and 
operational performance.
Engagement with investors is 
undertaken through multiple 
channels. During Q4 FY 2025–26, the 
Company participated in the Axis 
Investor Conference held in Mumbai, 
which facilitated interactions with 
both existing and potential investors. 
In addition, the Company’s dedicated 
Investor Relations function, together 
with members of the Senior 
Management Team, engaged with 
investors through a combination 
of in person and virtual meetings, 
reaching over 40+ investors during 
the financial year.
During the period, the Company 
also received enhanced analyst 
coverage, with approximately 10 
special coverage reports issued by 
reputed analyst firms, followed by 
over 16 preview reports, further 
strengthening market visibility and 
information dissemination.
INFORMATION 
TECHNOLOGY
As businesses navigate an era 
of rapid technological change, 
Cyient DLM continues to invest 
strategically in digital infrastructure, 
cybersecurity, and intelligent 
automation building the operational 
foundation for scalable, resilient, and 
future-ready manufacturing. During 
the year, the Company advanced key 
technology initiatives across smart 
manufacturing, artificial intelligence, 
and information security, reinforcing 
its position as a technology-led 
Electronics Manufacturing Services 
partner of choice.
Together, these initiatives reflect 
Cyient DLM’s unwavering focus on 
building secure, digital-first, and 
scalable operations creating an 
infrastructure that supports today’s 
commitments while laying the 
foundation for tomorrow’s growth.
SMART MANUFACTURING
The Company implemented a 
Manufacturing Execution System 
(MES) to enable real-time product 
tracking, end-to-end traceability, 
and enhanced operational visibility 
across its manufacturing processes. 
The MES platform bridges the gap 
between shop-floor execution 
and enterprise-level decision-
making, enabling faster response 
to production variances, improved 
quality control, and stronger on-time 
delivery performance, all critical to 
serving high-reliability customers 
in Aerospace & Defence, Medical 
Technology, and Industrial segments.
DATA ANALYTICS AND 
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE (AI)
The Company has initiated a range 
of advanced digital initiatives to 
embed artificial intelligence and 
automation across its operations. 
By leveraging AI platforms, 
including Microsoft Copilot and 
Claude, alongside Robotic Process 
Automation (RPA), the organisation 
is driving measurable improvements 
in workforce productivity, process 
efficiency, and the quality of business 
decision-making.
More broadly, AI is being adopted 
across functions including 
engineering, supply chain, and 
customer support to reduce manual 
effort, accelerate cycle times, and 
surface actionable insights from 
data. The Company views AI not as 
a point solution, but as a horizontal 
capability that will progressively 
permeate all areas of the business, 
enhancing competitiveness and 
enabling teams to focus on higher-
value work.
INFORMATION & 
CYBER SECURITY
In the Electronics Manufacturing 
Services industry, cybersecurity is not 
merely an IT concern but a business 
imperative. EMS companies handle 
sensitive customer intellectual 
property, proprietary design 
data, and classified programme 
information across complex, multi-
site supply chains. A security breach 
can result in irreparable reputational 
damage, regulatory penalties, and 
loss of customer trust, particularly 
in high-assurance sectors such as 
Aerospace & Defence and Medical 
Technology.
Cognisant of these risks, 
the Company undertook a 
comprehensive strengthening of 
its security posture during the year. 
Key measures included the upgrade 
of firewalls, data encryption, and 
endpoint security controls to protect 
proprietary intellectual property, as 
well as the implementation of Mobile 
Device Management (MDM) Security 
to govern and secure mobile access 
to corporate systems.
These investments delivered a 
tangible and independently verified 
outcome: Cyient DLM’s BitSight 
security rating improved from 740 
to 780, achieving an Advanced 
rating. This places the Company 
meaningfully ahead of the 720 
benchmark set for the EMS industry, 
a clear, external validation that 
the Company’s security controls 
exceed sector norms and meet the 
expectations of the most demanding 
global customers.

INFORMATION SECURITY 
MANAGEMENT SYSTEM
The Company successfully achieved 
ISO 27001:2022 re-certification, the 
globally recognised standard for 
Information Security Management 
Systems. ISO 27001:2022 certification 
signals to customers, partners, 
and regulators worldwide that 
the Company has implemented a 
robust, audited, and continuously 
improving framework for protecting 
sensitive information. For customers 
entrusting Cyient DLM with critical 
programme data and intellectual 
property, this certification provides 
independent assurance that their 
assets are managed to the highest 
international standards, serving 
as a meaningful differentiator in 
competitive programme bids and 
customer qualification processes.

CUL TURE
Having an inclusive culture is the cornerstone to creating a diverse, innovative environment that inspires growth and 
associate engagement. Cyient DLM defines its culture with the acronym AGILE (Ambition, Growth Mindset, Inclusive, 
Lead by Example, and Empowered).
We believe it is critical that we invest and focus on our culture to ensure that we have clear expectations of how we will 
lead, manage, act, and treat each other, our customers, and the community. This year we have created a focus on how 
we can ensure that inclusion is the key foundation for all our stakeholders to excel. This inclusive culture will ensure all 
of our associates have a voice and that it is heard and valued. With this the employer brand would increase and will help 
attract the right talent for our current and future requirements.
RESOURCE PLANNING
Resource planning is done based on 
anticipated future requirements. This 
planning takes into account specific 
project needs and the essential skill 
sets required. Understanding these 
factors significantly influences the 
talent acquisition process, aiding in 
the selection of suitable candidates, 
talent reviews, and facilitating 
learning and development initiatives.
With this we strive to have a 
process in place to ensure that the 
organisation has the right people 
in the right place at the right time 
aligning with our strategic goals.
POLICIES & PROCEDURE
Our policies provide our employees 
with clarity on how to conduct 
business ethically and responsibly. 
The policies of our company are 
reviewed periodically or on a 
need basis by the Board and its 
committees. During this assessment, 
the efficacy of the policies is 
reviewed and necessary changes 
to policies and procedures are 
implemented.
All our policies and procedural 
documents are available on the 
intranet for our associate’s reference.
PERFORMANCE MANAGEMENT
We believe in automation of our 
people management tools. Our 
online performance management 
tool gives us an edge to ensure 
our associates stay productive and 
engaged by having clear goal-
settings and open feedback.
A continuous process of evaluating 
associates will close the gaps, if any, 
by providing the necessary training 
to acquire required future skills & 
competencies.
LEARNING & DEVELOPMENT
We have an online tool for all 
learning needs that enables 
associates to develop the skills they 
need for the future. With this tool, 
we try to bridge the gaps between 
the resources we have today and 
those required in the near future. We 
understand the value of investing 
in our people to retain talent and 
provide them with opportunities to 
grow along with the organisation.
LEADERSHIP AND 
BEHAVIOURAL TRAINING
The Company invests in structured 
leadership and behavioural

initiatives reinforce leadership

while fostering a culture of 
accountability, teamwork, and 
sustained high performance.

1 Energy 
Management
Risk & 
Opportunity
Risk: Energy management is a significant risk due to its 
reliance on a stable energy supply. Disruptions such as 
power outages can severely impact production schedules 
and operational efficiency. Regulatory pressures 
to reduce carbon emissions and adopt sustainable 
practices can lead to substantial compliance costs and 
potential penalties for non-compliance. Key risks include 
dependence on grid electricity, energy cost fluctuations, 
and inefficient energy usage across operations.
Additionally, the volatility of energy prices makes it 
challenging to predict and manage operational costs, 
further exacerbating financial risks. The environmental 
footprint, including greenhouse gas emissions, can 
attract negative attention from stakeholders, potentially 
harming the company's reputation and market position.
Opportunity:  Energy management offers a major 
advantage. Adopting energy-efficient technologies 
reduces consumption and costs, boosting profitability and 
competitiveness. Shifting to renewables like solar ensures 
cost stability and shields against fossil fuel price swings. 
Companies excelling in energy management enhance 
their reputation, attracting eco-conscious investors and 
customers. Additionally, proactive strategies help meet 
environmental regulations, avoiding fines and unlocking 
government incentives for sustainable practices.
Cyient DLM addresses energy -related risks 
through a combination of mitigation and 
adaptation measures under its energy 
management approach. To mitigate these 
risks, the Company is increasing the share of 
renewable energy through adoption of solar 
power, leading to a reduction in dependence 
on conventional electricity sources. It has also 
implemented energy efficiency initiatives 
such as transition to LED lighting systems, 
installation of solar-powered infrastructure, 
and power optimization measures including 
power factor improvement.
In addition, operational initiatives such as 
process optimization, use of energy-efficient 
equipment, and deployment of solutions like 
motion sensor lighting contribute to reducing 
overall energy consumption.
These measures support improved energy 
efficiency, reduced environmental impact, and 
enhanced operational resilience.
Negative / 
Positive
2 Waste and 
Hazardous 
Materials 
Management
Risk Risk: Risk of inappropriate handling and disposal of 
hazardous waste, caused by insufficient training or 
lack of adherence to safety protocols. This may result 
in environmental pollution, health risks to employees 
and communities, and legal penalties. Compliance with 
regulations such as the ISO 14001 can help in avoiding 
fines and regulatory compliance. Employee training and 
engagement regarding waste management and disposal 
are also crucial.
Cyient DLM follows defined processes for 
management of waste and hazardous materials 
across its operations, in line with applicable 
regulatory requirements. Waste is segregated 
at source, handled safely, and disposed of 
through authorized channels, including 
disposal of hazardous waste in accordance with 
CPCB guidelines.

The Company has undertaken multiple 
initiatives to minimize waste generation 
and promote resource efficiency. These 
include elimination of single-use plastics 
and disposable items, implementation of

and adoption of reusable materials within 
operations. Such measures support reduction 
of landfill waste and improved circular use of 
resources.
To manage hazardous materials, Cyient DLM 
follows defined protocols for safe storage, 
handling, and usage in line with applicable 
safety and regulatory requirements. Efforts are 
also made to reduce dependence on hazardous 
substances through substitutions with safer 
alternatives and process optimization.
These practices support safe handling of wastes 
and chemicals while minimizing environmental

applicable regulations.

3 Green House 
Gases & 
Climate 
Change
Risk Risk:  The shift to a low-carbon economy presents 
significant risks due to its reliance on global supply chains 
and energy-intensive operations. Climate change can 
lead to severe weather events, disrupting supply chains 
and impacting raw material availability. Transitioning to 
renewable energy sources poses financial challenges, 
requiring substantial capital investment. Rapid 
innovation is necessary to keep pace with low-carbon 
technologies, posing risks for companies that lag. 
Additionally, climate change can exacerbate the scarcity 
of critical raw materials, increasing costs and supply 
chain vulnerabilities. Also, physical risks, such as extreme 
weather events and rising sea levels, can damage facilities 
and disrupt supply chains.
Cyient DLM addresses greenhouse gas (GHG) 
emissions and climate-related impacts through 
a combination of renewable energy adoption 
and energy efficiency measures across its 
operations. The Company has implemented 
solar power generation to reduce dependence 
on grid electricity, contributing to a reduction 
in Scope 2 emissions. In addition, initiatives 
such as transition to energy-efficient LED 
lighting systems, deployment of solar-powered 
infrastructure, and implementation of power 
optimization measures are undertaken to 
improve energy performance and minimize 
associated emissions.
Negative

4 Human 
capital 
Management
Opportunity Opportunity:  In the highly competitive landscape 
effective human capital and labour management present 
significant opportunities. Given the sensitivity to labour 
costs and the necessity to meet stringent production 
deadlines for new technology releases, maintaining 
positive relations is crucial. Poor management can lead 
to work stoppages and production disruptions, which not 
only reduce near-term revenue but also adversely affect 
long-term productivity due to diminished employee 
morale. By fostering good human relations, companies 
can mitigate these risks and ensure smooth operations. 
Furthermore, these practices enhance an entity's brand 
value and social license to operate, as they demonstrate 
a commitment to ethical standards and employee 
well-being. Additionally, investing in human capital 
through continuous training and development equips 
employees with the latest skills, fostering innovation and 
adaptability in a rapidly evolving industry. Thus, strategic 
human capital and labour management are not merely 
operational necessities but pivotal opportunities for 
resilience and competitiveness.

9 Supply chain 
Management
Risk & 
Opportunity
Risk: Supply chain management is fraught with risks due 
to its complexity and global reach. Geopolitical tensions, 
trade restrictions, and regulatory changes can disrupt 
the intricate network of suppliers and manufacturers. 
Rapid shifts in consumer demand and technological 
advancements make accurate forecasting difficult, 
leading to potential overproduction or stockouts. Natural 
disasters, transportation issues, and supplier insolvency 
further threaten supply continuity. Ensuring consistent 
quality is challenging, as defective parts can result in 
costly recalls and reputational damage. Price volatility 
of raw materials and components impacts production 
costs and profitability. Compliance with environmental 
regulations and ethical sourcing practices is essential to 
avoid legal penalties and maintain consumer trust.
Opportunity: Supply chain management offers 
significant opportunities by enhancing efficiency, 
reducing costs, and improving product quality. 
Streamlined processes reduce lead times and expedite 
production cycles, while optimized inventory levels and 
strategic sourcing lower costs. Robust quality control and 
supplier monitoring ensure consistent product reliability. 
Diversifying suppliers and establishing contingency 
plans mitigate risks, ensuring production continuity. By 
leveraging these opportunities, we can develop resilient 
and efficient supply chains.
To mitigate supply chain risks, we have adopted 
a comprehensive strategy. This includes 
diversifying our supplier base, implementing 
strategic sourcing, and optimizing inventory 
management practices. We leverage industry-
leading software like Kinaxis Rapid Response 
in supply chain to improve on-time delivery 
(OTD) and customer satisfaction. We are in the 
process of conducting regular assessments to 
ensure resilience and quality. There is a lot of 
focus and emphasis in terms of supplier on 
boarding and thereafter monitoring supplier 
performance Additionally, we have invested in 
tools, people, and processes to integrate and 
optimize business performance.
Negative / 
Positive
10 Data security 
and privacy
Risk Risk: Data security and privacy are critical due to the 
digitization and interconnectedness of processes. 
Protecting intellectual property (IP) is essential to prevent 
competitive disadvantages and financial losses. Ensuring 
operational continuity is vital, as cyberattacks like 
ransomware can disrupt production and cause significant 
damage. The interconnected supply chain amplifies these 
risks, with breaches potentially compromising the entire 
network. Regulatory compliance with data protection 
laws such as GDPR and CCPA are required, with non-
compliance leading to severe penalties. Cyberattacks, 
insider threats, and outdated systems pose significant 
risks.
Cyient DLM maintains a zero-violation standard 
for data privacy, we implement advanced data 
protection and backup systems to secure 
critical information. Our policies align with 
the stringent EU GDPR requirements, and our 
Information Security Management System 
(ISMS) is ISO/IEC 27001: 2022 certified. The 
effectiveness of our systems is reviewed half 
yearly by the ISMS internal audit team to 
ensure continuous improvement and robust 
protection.

Cyient DLM is dedicated to achieving excellence through a series of well-defined 
commitments, goals, and targets. Here are the key areas we are focusing on:
 • Implementing Carbon Footprint Reduction Initiatives:  We are actively working 
to reduce our carbon footprint through various initiatives aimed at minimizing 
emissions and promoting eco-friendly practices.
 • Enhancing the Use of Renewable Energy Sources: We are committed to increasing 
our reliance on renewable energy sources, thereby reducing our dependence on 
non-renewable resources and contributing to a greener planet.
 • Upholding a Zero-Violation Standard for Data Privacy Information:  We ensure 
the highest standards of data privacy through continuous monitoring and annual 
audits, adhering to compliance and safeguarding sensitive information.
Disclosure Questions P1 P2 P3 P4 P5 P6 P7 P8 P9
 • Increasing Community Engagement Programs:  We are dedicated to fostering 
strong relationships with the communities we serve by increasing our involvement 
in community engagement programs that address local needs and promote social 
well-being.
 • Achieving Gender Diversity: We strive to create a diverse and inclusive workplace 
by setting and achieving gender diversity targets, ensuring equal opportunities for 
all employees.
 • Continuous Employee Training & Development:  We prioritize the growth and 
development of our employees through continuous training programs that enhance 
their skills and knowledge, preparing them for future challenges.
 • Promoting Ethical Labour Practices and Fair Trade: We are committed to upholding 
ethical labor practices and promoting fair trade, ensuring that our operations are 
conducted with integrity and respect for human rights.
 • Strengthening Corporate Governance and Transparency: We aim to strengthen 
our corporate governance framework and enhance transparency in our operations, 
fostering trust and accountability among our stakeholders.
6. Performance of the entity against 
the specific commitments, goals 
and targets along-with reasons in 
case the same are not met.
We have undertaken several initiatives aimed at meeting our commitments, goals, and 
targets. Here are the key actions and their outcomes in FY 2025-26:
 • Installation of LED Lights: We have embarked on a significant initiative to replace 
outdated lighting fixtures with energy-efficient LED lights across all our sites. As of 
now, 100% of our sites are equipped with LED lighting. These lights not only reduce 
heat generation but also provide the required LUX levels, enhancing the effectiveness 
of air conditioning systems due to lower heat emissions.
 • Energy Audits: We conduct comprehensive energy audits for all critical equipment 
at our sites. Based on the audit reports, we initiate corrective actions and implement 
necessary steps to optimize power consumption, ensuring efficient energy use.
 • Renewable Energy: In FY 2025-26, renewable energy (RE) accounted for 63.61% of 
our total electricity consumption. We are aiming to increase RE utilization to 80% by 
expanding rooftop solar capacity and enhancing its adoption at our Mysuru facility.
 • Power Factor Correction for Better Energy Savings:   We have upgraded our 
capacitor bank with new capacitors to improve the power factor, contributing to 
better energy savings.
 • Installation of New Air Compressor: We have replaced conventional compressors 
with Variable Frequency Drive (VFD) controlled compressors that adjust their 
operation based on load, thereby reducing power consumption.
Governance, leadership, and oversight
7. Statement by director responsible 
for the business responsibility

The Electronics Manufacturing Services (EMS) industry is navigating a rapidly evolving 
landscape marked by significant Environmental, Social, and Governance challenges, 
including high carbon emissions, complex global supply chains, and increasing 
cybersecurity threats. At Cyient DLM, we view these challenges as catalysts for 
transformation and have embedded ESG principles at the core of our strategic vision.
Our environmental initiatives are focused on maximizing our renewable energy, reducing 
waste, and reducing water usage through ensuring we have rainwater harvesting on all 
of our locations. These efforts have led to a measurable reduction in our environmental 
footprint.

2. Does the entity have processes in place to avoid/ manage conflict of interests involving members of the Board? (Yes/No) 
If Yes, provide details of the same.
Yes, Cyient DLM has processes in place to identify, prevent and manage conflicts of interest involving members of the Board and 
Senior Management. As per the Code of Conduct for Board and Senior Management, individuals are required to avoid situations 
where personal interests may conflict, or appear to conflict, with the interests of Cyient DLM.
The Code requires disclosure of actual or potential conflicts to the Board, including matters such as external employment, board 
memberships, related party transactions, close personal relationships, financial investments, potential diversion of business 
opportunities, and use of Company assets.
These mechanisms support transparent governance and help ensure that decisions are taken in the best interests of Cyient DLM 
and its stakeholders.

2. A. Does the entity have procedures in place for sustainable sourcing? (Yes/No)
 B. If yes, what percentage of inputs were sourced sustainably?
Yes, Cyient DLM follows a structured approach to sustainable sourcing, integrating environmental and social considerations 
into supplier selection and evaluation processes.
Supplier assessment includes verification of recognized certifications such as ISO 45001, SA 8000, or OHSAS 18001, reflecting 
adherence to health, safety, and social responsibility standards.
Through these practices, Cyient DLM aims to strengthen supply chain resilience while promoting responsible and ethical 
sourcing behaviors.
3. Describe the processes in place to safely reclaim your products for reusing, recycling, and disposing at the end of life, for 
(a) Plastics (including packaging) (b) E-waste (c) Hazardous waste and (d) other waste.
Cyient DLM has established robust processes to manage end-of-life treatment of products, including mechanisms for safe 
reclamation, reuse, recycling, and disposal across applicable waste streams, in alignment with regulatory requirements and 
environmental best practices.
For plastics, the Company complies with applicable Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) requirements and engages authorized 
partners to procure green credits, offsetting its plastic footprint and supporting the responsible collection and recycling of plastic 
waste. Additionally, we have undertaken conscious efforts to eliminate plastic bottle usage across our facilities, significantly 
reducing costs, enhancing our brand image, and strengthening our ESG commitments. As a result, we have been able to avoid 
approximately 500 kg of plastic waste annually.

Hazardous waste generated at the site is stored and disposed of in accordance with the Hazardous and Other Wastes (Management 
and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016. The waste generated is handed over to authorized vendors for environmentally 
responsible disposal.
E-waste is similarly transferred to authorized recyclers in compliance with the E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022, ensuring 
proper handling and recycling in line with regulatory requirements. These initiatives contribute to environmentally sound waste 
management and reinforce Cyient DLM’s commitment to circular economy principles.
4. Whether Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) is applicable to the entity’s activities (Yes / No). If yes, whether the waste 
collection plan is in line with the Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) plan submitted to Pollution Control Boards? If 
not, provide steps taken to address the same.
Yes. Cyient DLM manages plastic waste in accordance with Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) requirements. The Company 
has engaged authorized consultants/agencies to procure green credits, enabling equivalent recovery of its plastic footprint 
through certified recyclers and authorized channels. Relevant data and disclosures are submitted to the Central Pollution Control 
Board (CPCB) to ensure regulatory compliance and transparency.

3. Accessibility of workplaces
Are the premises / offices of the entity accessible to differently abled employees and workers, as per the requirements of 
the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016? If not, whether any steps are being taken by the entity in this regard.
Cyient DLM ensures accessibility across its premises in line with the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016. 
Workplace infrastructure includes provisions such as ramps, elevators, and appropriate workspace arrangements to support 
employees with disabilities. The Company undertakes ongoing assessments of its facilities and addresses identified gaps to 
strengthen accessibility and promote an inclusive work environment.
4. Does the entity have an equal opportunity policy as per the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016? If so, provide a 
web-link to the policy.
Cyient DLM is committed to fostering an inclusive and equitable workplace through its Equal Opportunity Policy, which is aligned 
with the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016. The Policy ensures equal employment opportunities without discrimination 
on the basis of gender, religion, race, color, age, national origin, marital status, or disability. These principles are reinforced through 
the Company’s Code of Business Ethics and are implemented across its operations to promote a diverse and inclusive work 
environment. The Policy is accessible to all employees via the intranet portal.

10. Health and safety management system:
a. Whether an occupational health and safety management system has been implemented by the entity? (Yes/ No). If 
yes, the coverage such system?
b. What are the processes used to identify work-related hazards and assess risks on a routine and non-routine basis by 
the entity?
c. Whether you have processes for workers to report the work-related hazards and to remove themselves from such 
risks. (Y/N)
d. Do the employees/ worker of the entity have access to non-occupational medical and healthcare services? (Yes/ No)
(a) Yes, Cyient DLM follows a structured Occupational Health and Safety (OHS) Management System across its four sites 
to manage workplace health and safety risks. All the sites are certified to ISO 45001:2018, reflecting adherence to 
international standards on occupational health and safety management. The OHS framework enables consistent 
implementation of safety practices and supports a safe working environment across operations.
(b) Cyient DLM has established a systematic framework for identification and assessment of workplace hazards in line with 
ISO 45001:2018. This framework covers both routine and non routine activities across operations. Hazard Identification 
and Risk Assessment (HIRA) is conducted on a periodic basis, including annual exercises and updates linked to process 
changes, new equipment, or operational modifications. All incidents and near misses are documented and subject to 
Root Cause Analysis (RCA) to enable effective corrective and preventive actions. Risks are assessed based on defined 
criteria, and mitigation controls are implemented to minimize potential impacts and maintain safe working conditions.
(c) Yes, Cyient DLM has implemented an Incident Management Protocol supported by a digital Environmental, Health and 
Safety (EHS) reporting platform. The system enables employees to promptly report work related incidents, hazards, 
or near misses. In line with ISO 45001:2018 requirements, Cyient DLM has established Hazard Identification and Risk 
Assessment (HIRA) procedures to identify workplace hazards, assess associated risks, and implement appropriate 
control measures. These processes support systematic risk management and continual improvement in occupational 
health and safety performance. Reported incidents are automatically escalated to relevant teams, including fire and 
safety personnel, to ensure timely response and investigation. The process includes automated communication to the 
reporting employee, tracking of investigation progress, Root Cause Analysis (RCA), and implementation of corrective 
actions. Incidents are formally closed once actions are completed and relevant stakeholders are informed.
(d) Yes, employees and workers at Cyient DLM have access to non occupational medical and healthcare services. 
The Company facilitates general health check ups, medical consultations, and treatment support through tie ups with 
external healthcare providers and hospitals. In addition, periodic health assessments such as annual health check ups, 
pre employment medical screening, and diagnostic tests (including vision tests, audiometry, and blood investigations) 
are made available to support preventive healthcare. These initiatives enable employees to access essential healthcare 
services and support overall well being.

12. Describe the measures taken by the entity to ensure a safe and healthy workplace.
Cyient DLM is committed to maintaining a safe and healthy workplace through a structured Occupational Health and Safety (OHS) 
framework aligned with ISO 45001:2018 and implemented across all sites.
The Company undertakes regular Hazard Identification and Risk Assessment (HIRA) to identify workplace hazards and implement 
appropriate control measures. Safety training is provided at induction and through periodic programmes to reinforce awareness 
of safety protocols. Additional measures include air quality monitoring, periodic health screenings, adherence to machinery and 
electrical safety practices, and provision of appropriate personal protective equipment (PPE). These initiatives support effective 
risk mitigation and employee well being.

15. Provide details of any corrective action taken or underway to address safety-related incidents (if any) and on significant 
risks / concerns arising from assessments of health & safety practices and working conditions.
At present, Cyient DLM has not identified any major safety related incidents or significant risks requiring specific corrective actions 
during the reporting period. The Company continues to monitor workplace safety through its established OHS systems and 
processes to ensure timely identification and management of any emerging risks.
1. Does the entity extend any life insurance or any compensatory package in the event of death of (A) Employees (Y/N) (B) 
Workers (Y/N).
Yes, Cyient DLM provides life insurance and compensation support to employees and workers to safeguard their families in the 
event of demise. This includes coverage such as Group Term Life Insurance, Group Medical Insurance, and Group Personal Accident 
Insurance. In addition, the Company has an Employee Compensation Policy to provide financial assistance to the bereaved families. 
These measures aim to provide financial security and support during unforeseen circumstances.
2. Provide the measures undertaken by the entities to ensure that statutory dues have been deducted and deposited by the 
value chain partners.
Yes, Cyient DLM has established measures to ensure statutory compliance by its value chain partners.
Vendor agreements require suppliers to maintain accurate books and records in accordance with applicable laws and generally 
accepted accounting principles. In addition, the Vendor Business Code of Conduct mandates compliance with all relevant legal 
and regulatory requirements, including obligations relating to statutory deductions and payments. Compliance is supported 
through defined monitoring mechanisms, including periodic audits and verification processes. These measures enable oversight 
and promote transparency and accountability across the value chain.

4. Does the entity provide transition assistance programs to facilitate continued employability and the management of 
career endings resulting from retirement or termination of employment? (Yes/ No)
Yes, Cyient DLM has mechanisms to support employees during transition phases, including retirement and separation. The Company 
evaluates roles and business requirements to identify opportunities for engaging employees in contractual positions, where 
relevant. These measures support knowledge retention while facilitating a structured transition for employees.

6. Provide details of any corrective actions taken or underway to address significant risks / concerns arising from assessments 
of health and safety practices and working conditions of value chain partners.
Cyient DLM engages with its value chain partners to encourage adoption of environmentally responsible and safe operational 
practices. As part of this, the Company has supported suppliers in improving process efficiency and workplace safety, including 
the adoption of automated and ergonomically designed production lines (e.g., powder coating processes). Based on assessments 
of health, safety, and working conditions across value chain partners, no significant risks or concerns were identified during the 
reporting period. Accordingly, no specific corrective actions were required.

impact on operations and reputation. The Company maintains continuous engagement with stakeholders through defined 
communication and feedback mechanisms, enabling it to track expectations, respond to concerns, and support informed decision 
making. This approach strengthens stakeholder relationships and supports sustainable business performance.

1. Provide the processes for consultation between stakeholders and the Board on economic, environmental, and social topics 
or if consultation is delegated, how is feedback from such consultations provided to the Board.
Cyient DLM engages with its stakeholders on economic, environmental, and social matters to inform its ESG approach. The Company 
interacts with stakeholders through established channels such as meetings, surveys, and industry platforms to understand their 
expectations and concerns. Feedback from these engagements is reviewed and integrated into decision making processes. 
Where applicable, summaries of stakeholder inputs are shared with the Board to support informed oversight and alignment with 
business objectives and broader societal considerations. This approach enables Cyient DLM to align its operational priorities with 
stakeholder expectations and support sustainable business practices.
2. Whether stakeholder consultation is used to support the identification and management of environmental, and social 
topics (Yes / No). If so, provide details of instances as to how the inputs received from stakeholders on these topics were 
incorporated into policies and activities of the entity.
Yes, Cyient DLM undertakes stakeholder consultations to support identification and management of material environmental 
and social topics. The process includes development of a comprehensive list of ESG issues, assessment of their relevance to 
business operations and objectives, and evaluation through risk assessment frameworks. Stakeholder input is obtained through 
consultations and incorporated into the prioritization of issues based on their significance and the Company’s ability to address 
them. The identified priorities are integrated into the Company’s policies and operational practices to ensure alignment with 
stakeholder expectations and business objectives.
3. Provide details of instances of engagement with, and actions taken to, address the concerns of vulnerable/ marginalized 
stakeholder groups.
Cyient DLM engages with local communities, including vulnerable and marginalized groups, through initiatives focused on 
community development, environmental sustainability, skill development, and IT education.
The Company undertakes regular interactions to understand community needs and concerns, and implements programmes 
aligned with these priorities. Mechanisms are in place to address emerging issues in a timely manner, supporting effective 
community engagement and responsiveness.

4. Do you have a focal point (Individual/ Committee) responsible for addressing human rights impacts or issues caused or 
contributed to by the business? (Yes/No)
Yes, Cyient DLM has established mechanisms to address human rights impacts and concerns arising from its business activities. 
These responsibilities are embedded within the Company’s Grievance Redressal Policy, Discipline Policy, and Problem Resolution 
Policy. These frameworks provide formal channels for reporting, assessing, and resolving human rights related concerns, enabling 
systematic identification and mitigation of potential risks.
5. Describe the internal mechanisms in place to redress grievances related to human rights issues
Cyient DLM has established mechanisms to address and resolve human rights related grievances through its Grievance Redressal 
Policy, Discipline Policy, and Problem Resolution Policy. These policies provide structured processes for reporting, assessment, 
and resolution of concerns across the organization. Oversight is further supported through the Stakeholder Relationship 
Committee, which also functions as the Grievance Redressal Committee. In addition, the Company has a Whistle-Blower Policy 
that enables employees, including those in subsidiaries, to confidentially report unethical behavior or suspected violations to 
the Ombudsperson. The policy includes provisions to ensure confidentiality and protection against retaliation, supporting a 
transparent and accountable work environment.

8. Mechanisms to prevent adverse consequences to the complainant in discrimination and harassment cases.
Cyient DLM is committed to providing a workplace free from discrimination, gender bias, and sexual harassment. The Company has 
implemented a Prevention of Sexual Harassment (POSH) Policy, which is communicated to employees and outlines procedures for 
reporting, investigation, and resolution of complaints. Mechanisms are in place to ensure confidentiality, impartial investigation, 
and protection of complainants against retaliation or victimization. Complaints are addressed through defined processes by 
trained personnel, ensuring appropriate support and timely resolution.
9. Do human rights requirements form part of your business agreements and contracts? (Yes/No)
Yes, Cyient DLM incorporates human rights expectations within its engagement framework for vendors. Suppliers are required to 
adhere to internationally recognized human rights principles as outlined in the Vendor Business Code of Conduct and applicable 
contractual provisions. These requirements prohibit practices that are inconsistent with such standards and promote responsible 
conduct across the value chain.

implementation.
Yes, Cyient DLM has implemented Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD) mechanism at three of its operational sites. This ensures treatment 
and reuse of wastewater within the facility, minimizing discharge and supporting responsible water management practices. 
The remaining site currently operates with alternative wastewater management systems in compliance with applicable regulatory 
requirements.

8. Does the entity have any project related to reducing Green House Gas emission? If Yes, then provide details.
Yes, Cyient DLM has implemented projects focused on reduction of greenhouse gas emissions through renewable energy and 
energy efficiency improvements.
 Key initiatives include:
 • Installation of solar power at the Mysuru plant, targeting approximately a 30% reduction in dependence on grid electricity. 
In FY 2025–26, the plant achieved a 38% reduction in grid electricity usage compared to FY 2024–25.
 • Implementation of energy efficiency measures such as LED lighting and power optimization systems, resulting in reduced 
energy consumption across operations
 • Deployment of solar-powered street lighting, eliminating reliance on grid electricity for outdoor illumination
 These initiatives contribute to lowering Scope 2 emissions and improving overall environmental performance.

10. Briefly describe the waste management practices adopted in your establishments. Describe the strategy adopted by your 
company to reduce usage of hazardous and toxic chemicals in your products and processes and the practices adopted to 
manage such wastes.
Cyient DLM follows a structured waste management approach across its operations, including segregation, safe storage, authorized 
disposal of hazardous waste, and compliance with applicable regulatory requirements such as KSPCB guidelines.
 The Company has adopted measures to reduce dependency on hazardous chemicals, including:
 • Substitution with less hazardous alternatives, where feasible
 • Process optimization to reduce chemical usage and waste generation
 • Implementation of controlled storage and handling practices
 • Adherence to MSDS and regulatory protocols for safe handling
 These practices enable responsible chemical management and minimize environmental and health impacts from operations.

5. Does the entity have a business continuity and disaster management plan? Give details in 100 words/ web link.
Yes, Cyient DLM has established a Business Continuity and Disaster Management Plan to ensure continuity of operations and 
effective response to emergencies. The plan covers risk identification, impact assessment, and defined procedures for scenarios 
such as fire, natural disasters, and equipment failures. Roles and responsibilities are clearly defined, supported by trained 
Emergency Response Teams (ERT). Regular mock drills, inspections, and periodic reviews are conducted to assess effectiveness. 
Critical resources, communication protocols, and recovery strategies are in place to minimize disruption and enable timely 
restoration of operations while safeguarding employees, assets, and the environment.
6. Disclose any significant adverse impact to the environment, arising from the value chain of the entity. What mitigation 
or adaptation measures have been taken by the entity in this regard.
At present, Cyient DLM has not identified any significant adverse environmental impacts arising from its value chain during the 
reporting period. The Company continues to engage with its value chain partners to promote sustainable practices and compliance 
with environmental requirements. Monitoring mechanisms and periodic assessments are undertaken to identify potential risks, 
and appropriate mitigation measures are implemented where required.

3. Describe the mechanisms to receive and redress grievances of the community.
Cyient Foundation has established comprehensive grievance redressal mechanisms at Cyient DLM Adopted Schools and Cyient 
DLM Operated Rural Skill Centers. Grievance registers have been strategically placed to ensure easy accessibility for all stakeholders. 
These registers are designed to be language-appropriate and gender-sensitive, enabling inclusive participation. The system 
ensures that grievances receive prompt attention from the Cyient Foundation for swift resolution. Additionally, the Foundation 
has actively sensitized and educated the community on grievance redressal procedures, emphasizing timely and priority-based 
resolution of concerns.

1. Describe the mechanisms in place to receive and respond to consumer complaints and feedback.
Our organization has established robust mechanisms to effectively receive and respond to consumer complaints and feedback. 
Here is a detailed mechanisms that helps us ensure transparency, prompt resolution, and continuous improvement:
1. Primary Point of Contact: The Program Manager (PM) acts as the Single Point of Contact (SPOC) for all customer-related 
issues on a daily basis. The PM works in close collaboration with the DLM team to promptly address and resolve consumer 
complaints.
2. Quarterly Customer Reviews: We conduct quarterly reviews with our customers to foster continuous improvement and address 
any recurring concerns. These reviews help us stay aligned with customer expectations and enhance our service quality.
3. Escalation Matrix: An escalation matrix is in place to handle unresolved issues. If a complaint remains unresolved, it is escalated 
to the CEO for swift resolution and strategic oversight, ensuring that critical issues receive the highest level of attention.

1. A BRIEF OUTLINE ON THE COMPANY’S CORPORATE 
SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY (“CSR”) POLICY OF 
THE COMPANY:
a) About Company’s CSR Policy:
CYIENT DLM believes in giving back to society in a 
measure that is proportionate to its success in business. 
CYIENT DLM CSR activities are implemented through CYIENT 
FOUNDATION.
CYIENT DLM CSR activities are directed towards the benefit 
of different segments of society, specifically covering the 
deprived, underprivileged, and unemployed women/girls. 
It is aimed at providing equal opportunities and, thereby 
empowering them towards a dignified life.
CYIENT FOUNDATION (formerly Infotech Enterprises 
Charitable Trust) was executed on
Cyient Foundation (formerly Infotech Enterprises Charitable 
Trust) executed on March 27, 2002, at Hyderabad in the 
former united Andhra Pradesh by its Founder & Executive 
Chairman B. V. R. Mohan Reddy. Since its inception almost 
24 years ago, the Cyient Foundation has conceptualized and 
enabled various social initiatives.
Cyient Foundation is the corporate social responsibility 
(CSR) arm of Cyient India subsidiaries. Cyient Foundation's 
mission is to achieve long-term, holistic development of 
society by supporting programmes that bring sustainable 
change to the country. These include Education & IT Literacy, 
Skill Development & Employment, Business-aligned social 
innovation, Community & Environmental Development , and 
Healthcare Systems.
Cyient Foundation in accordance with its Charter and 
Mission Statement, works with communities, governments, 
civil society organizations and other organizations in India 
on various social welfare initiatives.
Cyient Foundation has been registered with GOVERNMENT 
OF INDIA, MINISTRY OF CORPORATE AFFAIRS for undertaking 
CSR activities in India with the Registration number 
“CSR00004617”. Cyient Foundation initiatives goes beyond 
charity and it works with the belief that as a responsible 
non profit organization, works on high impact community 
development programs and implementing sustainable 
social interventions.
Towards achieving long-term stakeholder value creation, 
CYIENT DLM shall always continue to respect the interests 
of and be responsive towards its key stakeholders - the 
communities, especially those from socially and economically 
backward groups, the underprivileged, marginalized, and 
most vulnerable groups; focused on girls and women in the 
society at large by providing equality in education and skills.
CYIENT DLM CSR programs aligned to national priorities 
and other international development goals like Sustainable 
Development Goals (SDGs), in line with Schedule VII of 
the Companies Act, 2013 as recommended by the CSR 
Committee of the Board and approved by the Board from 
time to time.
b) The Company’s CSR vision includes the following:
Ensuring socio-economic development of the community 
through different projects specifically covering the deprived, 
underprivileged children and unemployed women. Aimed at 
providing equal opportunities and thereby empowering 
them towards a dignified life and building a better 
tomorrow together. Ensuring environmental sustainability 
through green interventions, rainwater harvesting, and 
conservation projects.
c) The Company’s CSR mission
Achieving long-term, holistic development of the community 
around us by being committed to create and support 
programs that bring about sustainable changes through 
education, skill development, environmental protection, 
and innovation in healthcare systems.
Undertaking CSR Activities  Cyient DLM’s CSR activities 
are guided by its CSR committee & ESG Committee and CSR 
Policy and vision. The Company has formed a CSR committee 
(designated as CSR & ESG Committee) as per Section 135 
of the Companies Act, 2013 to formulate and recommend 
to the Board, a Corporate Social Responsibility Policy which 
shall indicate the activities to be undertaken by the Company 
as specified by law.

4. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY ALONG WITH THE DETAILS OF 
IMPACT ASSESSMENT OF CSR PROJECTS CARRIED OUT 
IN PURSUANCE OF SUB-RULE (3) OF RULE 8 OF THE 
COMPANIES (CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY 
POLICY) RULES, 2014:
As mandated by Sub-rule (3) of Rule 8 of the Companies 
(Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014., Cyient 
Foundation had conducted impact assessments for the 
Cyient DLM funded CSR projects. Rule 8 (3) provides that 
every company having CSR Obligation of C 10 Crore or 
more in pursuance of sub-section (5) of section 135 of the 
Act, in the 3 immediately preceding financial years, shall 
undertake impact assessment, through an independent 
agency, of the CSR projects having outlays of C 1 crore or 
more, and which have been completed not less than 1 year 
before undertaking. The impact assessment to be carried 
out project wise only in cases where both the conditions 
are fulfilled. In other cases, it can be taken up voluntarily. 
Cyient Foundation has voluntarily came up in conducting 
and publishing the impact assessment on the Cyient DLM 
CSR projects and appointed independent agencies.
Objects and purpose of Impact Assessments:
Cyient Foundation appointed Naipunya association for skill 
development & Digital Empowerment Foundation and 
Botlive Robotics for Robotics / STEM / Digital Education 
for execution and appointed Janma foundation to assess 
Cyient DLM funded CSR Projects in Education & Literacy and 
Empowering Women through Skill Development initiatives., 
The impact assessments aimed to evaluate the effectiveness 
of Cyient DLM CSR initiatives, identifying both the positive 
and negative impacts on various stakeholders, including the 
community, environment, and beneficiaries.
 • Projects Details:  Quality Education through school 
adoption and Women Empowerment through Skill 
Development.
 FY2025-26:
 • Cyient DLM supported 5 Government 
Schools through adoption
 • Cyient DLM supported Rural Micro 
Skill Centre in Mahabubanagar District, 
Telangana, India.
 • Objective of the study and assessment:
 Assess whether the CSR Interventions of Cyient 
Foundation serves the desired purpose for 
the school children / community / public / the 
environment.
 Track the grievances if any aroused through the 
interventions.
 • Key findings of the independent impact assessments:
a) Contribution to Education & Digital 
Literacy – FY26
The study has attempted to assess the impact 
of the Cyient DLM CSR initiatives in Education 
and Digital Literacy Programs through various 
analytical approaches. It has observed the various 
factors that have helped / hindered the program 
to achieve the desired outcomes.
(i) Cyient DLM CSR Interventions through 
Cyient Foundation serving the core purpose 
for the school children / community / public
(ii) It has the positive social impact in terms 
of providing quality education to the 
deprived communities , girl child enrolment 
has increased and pass percentage has 
improved in Cyient DLM Adopted schools.

Annexure - H
CONSERVATION OF ENERGY , TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION, AND 
FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS & OUTGO
1. CONSERVATION OF ENERGY
At Cyient DLM, energy conservation is a key focus area aligned with our commitment to sustainable operations and environmental 
responsibility. The company continuously undertakes initiatives to optimise energy consumption, improve efficiency, and reduce 
its carbon footprint across all operational facilities.
 Key initiatives implemented during the reporting period include:
 • We have commissioned a 500 kVA solar power plant at Mysuru Plant-1, enabling the facility to operate on approximately 70% 
renewable energy. Additionally, the Abhitech Park facility is powered predominantly by solar energy, with around 85% of its 
total energy consumption sourced from renewable energy.
 • Implementation of energy-efficient lighting solutions, including LED fixtures across manufacturing units and office spaces, 
along with installation of solar-powered lighting poles in outdoor areas to reduce conventional energy consumption.
 • Optimisation of HVAC systems through periodic maintenance, temperature standardisation, inverted type AC and use of 
energy-efficient equipment.
 • Installation and effective utilisation of timers to minimise unnecessary energy consumption.
 • Regular monitoring of energy consumption patterns to identify improvement opportunities and reduce wastage.
 • Awareness programmes and employee engagement initiatives to promote energy-saving practices at the workplace.
 • Preventive maintenance of machinery and utilities to ensure optimal performance and reduced energy losses.
 Future Plans/Commitments:
 • Exploring the adoption of renewable energy sources such as solar power to reduce dependency on conventional energy for 
Bangalore and Hyderabad locations.
 • Implementation of advanced energy management systems for real-time monitoring and control.
 • Setting measurable targets for reduction in energy intensity across operations.
 • Continuous improvement through adoption of best practices and energy-efficient technologies.
2. WASTE MANAGEMENT PROGRAMME:
 Key initiatives undertaken during the reporting period include:
 • Ensuring full compliance with regulatory requirements by partnering with certified waste disposal vendors authorised by the 
Pollution Control Board.
 • Implementation of structured waste segregation practices at source to enable efficient recycling and safe disposal.
 • Conducting training programmes for housekeeping staff on effective segregation and management of garden and food waste.
 • Adoption of vermicomposting practices to convert biodegradable waste into nutrient-rich compost. The compost generated 
is effectively utilised for in-house landscaping activities, promoting circular resource use.
 • Enhancement of rainwater harvesting infrastructure, with storage capacity increased from 18 KL to 28 KL, supporting water 
conservation and sustainable resource management.
 • Switching to recycled paper and sustainable pens is a practical next step after reducing plastic bottles. After the pen refill is 
finished, instead of discarding it as waste, employees can plant the pen in soil. The back end of the pen contains a small seed 
capsule. When placed in soil and watered regularly, it can grow into plants.
 Future Focus Areas:
 • Strengthening waste minimisation initiatives through increased recycling and reuse practices.
 • Improving data tracking and reporting of waste generation and disposal.
 • Expanding awareness programmes to drive employee participation in sustainable waste management practices.
3. GO-GREEN DRIVE
At Cyient DLM, the Go-Green Drive reflects our commitment to environmental sustainability through afforestation, emission 
reduction, and employee engagement initiatives across all operational locations.
 Key initiatives undertaken during the reporting period include:
 • Implementation of afforestation drives across our facilities to enhance green cover and promote ecological balance.
 • On the occasion of World Environment Day, a “Gift a Plant” initiative was conducted for employees across our Hyderabad, 
Bangalore, and Mysore locations to encourage individual participation in environmental conservation.
 • Organisation of pollution check drives at multiple sites, focusing on monitoring vehicular emissions.
 • Conducting dedicated campaigns to assess carbon emissions from employee-owned two-wheelers and four-wheelers, 
promoting awareness on emission control and responsible transportation practices.
 • The Company has taken steps to reduce plastic bottle usage across its facilities, supporting waste reduction and broader 
ESG goals.
 Future Focus Areas:
 • Expanding plantation drives with measurable targets for increasing green cover.
 • Encouraging the use of low-emission and sustainable transport options among employees by providing charging station 
within the company premises.
 • Strengthening periodic emission monitoring and awareness programmes.
4. TECHNOLOGY DRIVE:
 • Deployment of a Visitor Management System (VMS) to ensure efficient visitor tracking, improved security, and reduced 
paper usage.
 Future Focus Areas – Technology Drive
 • Integration of advanced technologies such as centralised dashboards and analytics for real-time monitoring and 
decision-making.
 • Implementation of smart access control and enhanced Visitor Management Systems with automation and data integration 
capabilities.
 • Adoption of IoT-based solutions for efficient facility management, including energy and utility monitoring.
 • Strengthening data security, system reliability, and compliance through robust IT infrastructure and governance frameworks.

1 . COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CORPORATE 
GOVERNANCE
At Cyient DLM, Corporate Governance is anchored in the 
Group’s “Value First” philosophy, which places the creation 
of long-term, sustainable value at the core of every decision 
and action. As a Cyient Group Company, we are guided by 
this principle to consistently prioritise stakeholder value, 
ethical conduct, and responsible growth.
Our approach to governance reflects a forward-looking 
mindset that aligns business performance with 
accountability and trust. We believe that strong governance 
is fundamental to building enduring relationships with our 
stakeholders shareholders, employees, customers, partners, 
and the communities we serve while ensuring resilience in a 
dynamic business environment.
Corporate Governance at Cyient DLM is not viewed as a 
mere framework of compliance, but as a strategic enabler 
that strengthens organisational integrity and supports value 
creation. It drives us to uphold high standards of ethics, 
operate within the legal and regulatory framework, and adopt 
progressive practices that go beyond prescribed norms.
Our governance framework is institutionalised through 
a comprehensive Code of Conduct and Ethics, robust 
governance policies, and clearly articulated Board and 
Committee charters. The effectiveness of this framework 
is demonstrated through the functioning of our Board, the 
discipline of our management processes, and the strength 
of our audit and internal control mechanisms.
The principles of governance are deeply embedded into 
our business strategy and day-to-day operations. Our key 
governance pillars include:
Value-Centric Transparency: We emphasise clear, accurate, 
and timely disclosures that enable stakeholders to make 
informed decisions and build lasting trust.
Ownership and Accountability:  Strong governance 
structures ensure that the Board and management take 
responsibility for outcomes while safeguarding stakeholder 
interests.
Ethical Foundation:  Integrity underpins all our actions, 
driving a culture of honesty, fairness, and strict adherence 
to legal and regulatory requirements.
Equity and Respect: We are committed to fair and inclusive 
treatment of all stakeholders, acknowledging diverse 
perspectives and fostering mutual respect.
Proactive Risk Stewardship: We integrate risk management 
into business decisions to protect and enhance long-term 
value and organisational resilience.
Sustainable Value Creation:  In line with our Value First 
approach, we embed environmental, social, and governance 
considerations into our growth strategy.
Stakeholder-Centric Engagement:  We actively engage 
with stakeholders and incorporate their expectations into 
our strategic and operational decisions.
Independent Oversight: A diverse and independent Board 
provides balanced judgement, effective oversight, and 
constructive challenge to management.
Principled Compliance:  While ensuring adherence to all 
applicable laws and standards, we remain committed to 
elevating governance practices beyond compliance.

with Regulation 17 of the SEBI Listing Regulations.
I. BOARD COMPOSITION AND CATEGORY OF DIRECTORS

Non-Executive, and Independent Directors to maintain the 
independence of the Board and separate the functions of 
governance and management. The composition of the Board

including 1 (one) Executive Director, 2 (two) Non-Executive 
Non-Independent Directors, 5 (five) Independent Directors, 
including 1 (one) woman Independent Director.
All Independent Directors possess the requisite qualifications 
and are experienced in their respective professional fields.

independence, and knowledge required for the Board to be 
effective.
II. MEETINGS OF THE BOARD
The Board meets at regular intervals to deliberate on 
strategic, operational, and regulatory matters concerning 
the Company. Directors devote adequate time and attention 
in preparation for and participation in the meetings, 
enabling effective contribution to Board deliberations and 
decision-making. The Board is supported by comprehensive 
governance policies, procedures, and systems that facilitate 
transparency, accountability, and informed decision-making.
During the financial year FY26, the Board of Directors met 
six (6) times, on April 22, 2025; July 1, 2025; July 22, 2025; 
October 14, 2025; October 24, 2025; and January 20, 2026.
The gap between any two consecutive Board meetings did 
not exceed one hundred and twenty days, in compliance 
with the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and the 
SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015. The Company is also in compliance with 
Secretarial Standard-1 on Board Meetings issued by ICSI.
A separate meeting of Independent Directors was held on 
20 April 2026, without the presence of Executive Directors 
or management.
The necessary quorum was present at all the meetings. 
During the year, three (3) resolutions were also passed by 
circulation in accordance with applicable provisions of law.

H. BOARD EVALUATION AND ASSESSMENT
The Board of Directors has carried out an annual evaluation 
of its own performance, that of its committees and 
individual Directors, in accordance with the provisions of 
the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015.
The evaluation framework is designed to enhance Board 
effectiveness and serves as a structured mechanism for 
continuous improvement. It encompasses key aspects such 
as Board composition, governance role, effectiveness of 
processes, quality of information flow, strategic oversight, 
and risk management.
During FY26, the Company conducted a formal and 
structured Board evaluation exercise through an online 
assessment process. The evaluation was carried out based on 
criteria aligned with the Guidance Note on Board Evaluation 
issued by SEBI and included assessment of the Board as a 
whole, its Committees, the Chairperson, the Managing 
Director, and individual Directors.
The process was conducted after obtaining inputs from all 
Directors and included parameters such as effectiveness 
of Board and Committee deliberations, clarity of roles and 
responsibilities, engagement and participation, and overall 
governance effectiveness. The evaluation also included 
assessment of the independence of the Independent 
Directors and their compliance with the prescribed criteria.
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee, under the 
stewardship of its Independent Chairperson, oversaw the 
evaluation process. The outcome of the evaluation was 
reviewed by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee 
and subsequently presented to the Board.
I. FAMILIARISATION PROGRAMMES
Non-Executive Directors inducted on the board are given 
an orientation about the Company, its operations, services, 
board procedures, and processes.
Newly inducted Directors spend approximately 2-3 days at 
the time of their induction and interact with the Chairman, 
Managing Director, CEO, CFO, and other members of the 
Senior Management. They interact with the heads of business 
units and other functional heads. They are provided with a 
walk-through of the units/factories and meet the factory 
managers. Directors are regularly updated on changes in 
policies and programmes, laws, and the general business 
environment. Details of the familiarisation programme for 
Non-Executive Directors and their letter of appointment are 
published on the website of the Company in the link: https://
www.cyientdlm.com/investors/corporate-governance
J SUCCESSION PLANNING AT THE BOARD AND SENIOR 
MANAGEMENT LEVELS
The Company adopts a structured succession management 
and planning framework to identify and develop future 
leaders, enabling it to effectively address the challenges 
associated with growth. For a well-governed Board, a clearly 
defined succession plan that guides the identification and 
selection of potential Board members with the requisite 
competencies is essential. Such a framework facilitates 
timely and systematic induction of new Directors, ensuring 
continuity in Board functioning without disruption and 
enabling the Company to respond effectively to business 
exigencies. The Nomination & Remuneration Committee 
(NRC)is entrusted with overseeing Board succession planning.

issues, and ensuring expedient resolution of diverse matters. 
The Committees also make specific recommendations to the 
Board on various matters when required. All observations, 
recommendations, and decisions of the Committees are 
placed before the Board for information or approval.
